recommendation_versions:
- version_id: REC-20260909-7112e7
  number: 1
  content: '## Working position


    The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
    close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
    Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
    to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


    The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
    its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records
    are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also
    remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification or
    account restrictions difficult to support.


    Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends
    at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users
    receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once
    its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


    ## Plausible alternatives


    A narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC
    evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and monitoring
    validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if state licensing,
    AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.


    ## Material facts and assumptions


    Reported facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely
    on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance, and the
    unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both entities conduct regulated
    money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will become the operating provider
    immediately after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or
    that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.


    ## What could change this view


    The view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path for
    each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate records
    or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing shows
    no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.


    ## Support limit


    This is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No external authority
    was retrieved.'

  content: '### Saved overall view


    The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
    close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
    Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
    to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


    The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
    its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records
    are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also
    remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification or
    account restrictions difficult to support.


    Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends
    at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users
    receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once
    its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.

    **Working position**


    The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
    close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
    Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
    to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


    The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
    its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records
    are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also
    remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification or
    account restrictions difficult to support.


    Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends
    at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users
    receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once
    its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


    **Plausible alternatives**


    A narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC
    evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and monitoring
    validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if state licensing,
    AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.


    **Material facts and assumptions**


    Reported facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely
    on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance, and the
    unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both entities conduct regulated
    money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will become the operating provider
    immediately after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or
    that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.


    **What could change this view**


    The view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path for
    each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate records
    or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing shows
    no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.


    **Support limit**


    This is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No external authority
    was retrieved.


    </details>'

        - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c
          source_hash: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5
          source_version: 73e298dedf82a3cd3cd03c2de42b05a91271b6008cfa33f0d1177846a54233f5
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md
          title: request.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '# Original Request


            From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

            Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


            We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
            plan:


            240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1
            post-close. One unified account, one balance, full access to our product
            set.

            No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice
            win, opens four new markets.

            Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
            results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
            before 2023.

            Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at
            close. Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day
            1.

            Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs
            from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


            Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

            '
          selected_passages:
          - start: 0
            end: 1095
            text: '# Original Request


              From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

              Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


              We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
              plan:


              240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day
              1 post-close. One unified account, one balance, full access to our product
              set.

              No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

              Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice
              win, opens four new markets.

              Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
              results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
              before 2023.

              Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
              at close. Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on
              day 1.

              Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
              launching close+1.

              Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of
              SARs from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


              Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

              '

              Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


              Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


              CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


              PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


              Subpart B—Programs


              § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


              31 CFR § 1022.210 - Anti-money laundering programs for money services
              businesses.


              CFR


              prev | next


              § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


              (a) Each money services business, as defined by § 1010.100(ff) of this
              chapter, shall develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money
              laundering program. An effective anti-money laundering program is one
              that is reasonably designed to prevent the money services business from
              being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist
              activities.


              (b) The program shall be commensurate with the risks posed by the location
              and size of, and the nature and volume of the financial services provided
              by, the money services business.


              (c) The program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall
              make copies of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection
              to the Department of the Treasury upon request.


              (d) At a minimum, the program shall:


              (1) Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably
              designed to assure compliance with this chapter.


              (i) Policies, procedures, and internal controls developed and implemented
              under this section shall include provisions for complying with the requirements
              of this chapter including, to the extent applicable to the money services
              business, requirements for:


              (A) Verifying customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph
              (d)(1)(iv) of this section;


              (B) Filing Reports;


              (C) Creating and retaining records;


              (D) Responding to law enforcement requests.


              (ii) Money services businesses that have automated data processing systems
              should integrate their compliance procedures with such systems.


              (iii) A person that is a money services business solely because it is
              an agent for another money services business as set forth in § 1022.380(a)(3)
              , and the money services business for which it serves as agent, may
              by agreement allocate between them responsibility for development of
              policies, procedures, and internal controls required by this paragraph
              (d)(1). Each money services business shall remain solely responsible
              for implementation of the requirements set forth in this section, and
              nothing in this paragraph (d)(1) relieves any money services business
              from its obligation to establish and maintain an effective anti-money
              laundering program.


              (iv) A money services business that is a provider or seller of prepaid
              access must establish procedures to verify the identity of a person
              who obtains prepaid access under a prepaid program and obtain identifying
              information concerning such a person , including name, date of birth,
              address, and identification number. Sellers of prepaid access must also
              establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains
              prepaid access to funds that exceed $10,000 during any one day and obtain
              identifying information concerning such a person , including name, date
              of birth, address, and identification number. Providers of prepaid access
              must retain access to such identifying information for five years after
              the last use of the prepaid access device or vehicle; such information
              obtained by sellers of prepaid access must be retained for five years
              from the date of the sale of the prepaid access device or vehicle.


              (2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program
              and this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall include
              assuring that:


              (i) The money services business properly files reports, and creates
              and retains records, in accordance with applicable requirements of this
              chapter;


              (ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect current
              requirements of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department
              of the Treasury ; and


              (iii) The money services business provides appropriate training and
              education in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.


              (3) Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning
              their responsibilities under the program, including training in the
              detection of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services
              business is required to report such transactions under this chapter.


              (4) Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate
              program. The scope and frequency of the review shall be commensurate
              with the risk of the financial services provided by the money services
              business. Such review may be conducted by an officer or employee of
              the money services business so long as the reviewer is not the person
              designated in paragraph (d)(2) of this section.'

            § 1022.210 Anti-money laundering programs for money services businesses.


            (a) Each money services business, as defined by § 1010.100(ff) of this
            chapter, shall develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money
            laundering program. An effective anti-money laundering program is one
            that is reasonably designed to prevent the money services business from
            being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist
            activities.


            (b) The program shall be commensurate with the risks posed by the location
            and size of, and the nature and volume of the financial services provided
            by, the money services business.


            (c) The program shall be in writing, and a money services business shall
            make copies of the anti-money laundering program available for inspection
            to the Department of the Treasury upon request.


            (d) At a minimum, the program shall:


            (1) Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably
            designed to assure compliance with this chapter.


            (i) Policies, procedures, and internal controls developed and implemented
            under this section shall include provisions for complying with the requirements
            of this chapter including, to the extent applicable to the money services
            business, requirements for:


            (A) Verifying customer identification, including as set forth in paragraph

            (B) Filing Reports;


            (C) Creating and retaining records;


            (D) Responding to law enforcement requests.


            (ii) Money services businesses that have automated data processing systems
            should integrate their compliance procedures with such systems.


            (iii) A person that is a money services business solely because it is
            an agent for another money services business as set forth in § 1022.380(a)(3)

            agreement allocate between them responsibility for development of policies,
            procedures, and internal controls required by this paragraph (d)(1). Each
            money services business shall remain solely responsible for implementation
            of the requirements set forth in this section, and nothing in this paragraph

            and maintain an effective anti-money laundering program.


            (iv) A money services business that is a provider or seller of prepaid
            access must establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who
            obtains prepaid access under a prepaid program and obtain identifying
            information concerning such a person , including name, date of birth,
            address, and identification number. Sellers of prepaid access must also
            establish procedures to verify the identity of a person who obtains prepaid
            access to funds that exceed $10,000 during any one day and obtain identifying
            information concerning such a person , including name, date of birth,
            address, and identification number. Providers of prepaid access must retain
            access to such identifying information for five years after the last use
            of the prepaid access device or vehicle; such information obtained by
            sellers of prepaid access must be retained for five years from the date
            of the sale of the prepaid access device or vehicle.


            (2) Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program
            and this chapter. The responsibilities of such person shall include assuring
            that:


            (i) The money services business properly files reports, and creates and
            retains records, in accordance with applicable requirements of this chapter;


            (ii) The compliance program is updated as necessary to reflect current
            requirements of this chapter, and related guidance issued by the Department
            of the Treasury ; and


            (iii) The money services business provides appropriate training and education
            in accordance with paragraph (d)(3) of this section.


            (3) Provide education and/or training of appropriate personnel concerning
            their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection
            of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services business
            is required to report such transactions under this chapter.


            (4) Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate
            program. The scope and frequency of the review shall be commensurate with
            the risk of the financial services provided by the money services business.

            business so long as the reviewer is not the person designated in paragraph

              Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


              Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


              CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


              PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


              Subpart C—Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses


              § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


              31 CFR § 1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious
              transactions.


              CFR


              Table of Popular Names


              prev | next


              § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


              (a)  General.


              (1) Every money services business described in § 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3),
              (4), (5), (6), and (7) of this chapter, shall file with the Treasury
              Department, to the extent and in the manner required by this section,
              a report of any suspicious transaction relevant to a possible violation
              of law or regulation. Any money services business may also file with
              the Treasury Department, by using the form specified in paragraph (b)(1)
              of this section, or otherwise, a report of any suspicious transaction
              that it believes is relevant to the possible violation of any law or
              regulation but whose reporting is not required by this section.


              (2) A transaction requires reporting under the terms of this section
              if it is conducted or attempted by, at, or through a money services
              business, involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000
              (except as provided in paragraph (a)(3) of this section), and the money
              services business knows, suspects, or has reason to suspect that the
              transaction (or a pattern of transactions of which the transaction is
              a part):


              (i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or conducted
              in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal activity
              (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location,
              or control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or
              evade any Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting
              requirement under Federal law or regulation;


              (ii) Is designed, whether through structuring or other means, to evade
              any requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations promulgated
              under the Bank Secrecy Act ; or


              (iii) Serves no business or apparent lawful purpose, and the reporting
              money services business knows of no reasonable explanation for the transaction
              after examining the available facts, including the background and possible
              purpose of the transaction .


              (iv) Involves use of the money services business to facilitate criminal
              activity.


              (3) To the extent that the identification of transactions required to
              be reported is derived from a review of clearance records or other similar
              records of money orders or traveler''s checks that have been sold or
              processed, an issuer of money orders or traveler''s checks shall only
              be required to report a transaction or pattern of transactions that
              involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $5,000.


              (4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report a suspicious
              transaction rests with each money services business involved in the
              transaction , provided that no more than one report is required to be
              filed by the money services businesses involved in a particular transaction
              (so long as the report filed contains all relevant facts). Whether,
              in addition to any liability on its own for failure to report, a money
              services business that issues the instrument or provides the funds transfer
              service involved in the transaction may be liable for the failure of
              another money services business involved in the transaction to report
              that transaction depends upon the nature of the contractual or other
              relationship between the businesses, and the legal effect of the facts
              and circumstances of the relationship and transaction involved, under
              general principles of the law of agency.


              (b)  Filing procedures —(1) What to file. A suspicious transaction shall
              be reported by completing a Suspicious Activity Report(“SAR”), and collecting
              and maintaining supporting documentation as required by paragraph (c)
              of this section.


              (2)  Where to file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to
              be determined by FinCEN , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.'

            Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


            Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


            CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


            PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


            Subpart C—Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses


            § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


            31 CFR § 1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious
            transactions.


            CFR


            Table of Popular Names


            prev | next


            § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


            (a)  General.


            (1) Every money services business described in § 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3),
            (4), (5), (6), and (7) of this chapter, shall file with the Treasury Department,
            to the extent and in the manner required by this section, a report of
            any suspicious transaction relevant to a possible violation of law or
            regulation. Any money services business may also file with the Treasury
            Department, by using the form specified in paragraph (b)(1) of this section,
            or otherwise, a report of any suspicious transaction that it believes
            is relevant to the possible violation of any law or regulation but whose
            reporting is not required by this section.


            (2) A transaction requires reporting under the terms of this section if
            it is conducted or attempted by, at, or through a money services business,
            involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000 (except
            as provided in paragraph (a)(3) of this section), and the money services
            business knows, suspects, or has reason to suspect that the transaction
            (or a pattern of transactions of which the transaction is a part):


            (i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or conducted
            in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal activity
            (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location,
            or control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or evade
            any Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting requirement
            under Federal law or regulation;


            (ii) Is designed, whether through structuring or other means, to evade
            any requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations promulgated
            under the Bank Secrecy Act ; or


            (iii) Serves no business or apparent lawful purpose, and the reporting
            money services business knows of no reasonable explanation for the transaction
            after examining the available facts, including the background and possible
            purpose of the transaction .


            (iv) Involves use of the money services business to facilitate criminal
            activity.


            (3) To the extent that the identification of transactions required to
            be reported is derived from a review of clearance records or other similar
            records of money orders or traveler''s checks that have been sold or processed,
            an issuer of money orders or traveler''s checks shall only be required
            to report a transaction or pattern of transactions that involves or aggregates
            funds or other assets of at least $5,000.


            (4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report a suspicious
            transaction rests with each money services business involved in the transaction
            , provided that no more than one report is required to be filed by the
            money services businesses involved in a particular transaction (so long
            as the report filed contains all relevant facts). Whether, in addition
            to any liability on its own for failure to report, a money services business
            that issues the instrument or provides the funds transfer service involved
            in the transaction may be liable for the failure of another money services
            business involved in the transaction to report that transaction depends
            upon the nature of the contractual or other relationship between the businesses,
            and the legal effect of the facts and circumstances of the relationship
            and transaction involved, under general principles of the law of agency.


            (b)  Filing procedures —(1) What to file. A suspicious transaction shall
            be reported by completing a Suspicious Activity Report(“SAR”), and collecting
            and maintaining supporting documentation as required by paragraph (c)
            of this section.


            (2)  Where to file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to be
            determined by FinCEN , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.'

              Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


              Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


              CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


              PART 1010—GENERAL PROVISIONS


              Subpart D—Records Required To Be Maintained


              § 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.


              31 CFR § 1010.430 - Nature of records and retention period.


              CFR


              prev | next


              § 1010.430 Nature of records and retention period.


              (a) Wherever it is required that there be retained either the original
              or a copy or reproduction of a check, draft, monetary instrument , investment
              security , or other similar instrument, there shall be retained a copy
              of both front and back of each such instrument or document, except that
              no copy need be retained of the back of any instrument or document which
              is entirely blank or which contains only standardized printed information,
              a copy of which is on file.


              (b) Records required by this chapter to be retained by financial institutions
              may be those made in the ordinary course of business by a financial
              institution . If no record is made in the ordinary course of business
              of any transaction with respect to which records are required to be
              retained by this chapter, then such a record shall be prepared in writing
              by the financial institution .


              (c) The rules and regulations issued by the Internal Revenue Service
              under 26 U.S.C. 6109 determine what constitutes a taxpayer identification
              number and whose number shall be obtained in the case of an account
              maintained by one or more persons.


              (d) All records that are required to be retained by this chapter shall
              be retained for a period of five years. Records or reports required
              to be kept pursuant to an order issued under § 1010.370 of this chapter
              shall be retained for the period of time specified in such order, not
              to exceed five years. All such records shall be filed or stored in such
              a way as to be accessible within a reasonable period of time, taking
              into consideration the nature of the record, and the amount of time
              expired since the record was made.


              [ 75 FR 65812 , Oct. 26, 2010, as amended at 81 FR 76864 , Nov. 4, 2016]

          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '# Known Facts


            - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
            out).

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance
            and full access to Mosaic''s product set.

            - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
            Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
            pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at
            Mosaic''s engine on day 1.

            - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

            - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were
            already KYC''d.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
            results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
            Relay''s engine on day 1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

            - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
            buys Harbor''s assets)

            - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
            institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

            - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
            last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
            Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its
            own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

            - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
            is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
            and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s
            AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must
            confirm.

            - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
            been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
            have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
            is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before
            onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume
            Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


            ## Assumptions


            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and
            Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition
            without new state approval

            - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
            Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

            - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
            validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state
            MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
            41 states (per the request).

            - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
            which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
            (structure not yet confirmed).

            '

              buys Harbor''s assets)

              - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
              institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
              required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

              - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
              last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

              - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
              Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for
              its own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

              - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
              is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic
              Relay''s AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance
              must confirm.

              - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
              have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

              - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to
              use or assume Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


              ## Assumptions


              - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses
              and Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the
              acquisition without new state approval

              - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
              Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

              - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
              validated before close without a monitoring gap

              - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to
              state MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

              - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
              41 states (per the request).

              - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
              which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
              (structure not yet confirmed).

              '

              Title 31—Money and Finance: Treasury


              Subtitle B—Regulations Relating to Money and Finance


              CHAPTER X—FINANCIAL CRIMES ENFORCEMENT NETWORK, DEPARTMENT OF THE TREASURY


              PART 1022—RULES FOR MONEY SERVICES BUSINESSES


              Subpart C—Reports Required To Be Made By Money Services Businesses


              § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


              31 CFR § 1022.320 - Reports by money services businesses of suspicious
              transactions.


              CFR


              Table of Popular Names


              prev | next


              § 1022.320 Reports by money services businesses of suspicious transactions.


              (a)  General.


              (1) Every money services business described in § 1010.100(ff)(1) , (3),
              (4), (5), (6), and (7) of this chapter, shall file with the Treasury
              Department, to the extent and in the manner required by this section,
              a report of any suspicious transaction relevant to a possible violation
              of law or regulation. Any money services business may also file with
              the Treasury Department, by using the form specified in paragraph (b)(1)
              of this section, or otherwise, a report of any suspicious transaction
              that it believes is relevant to the possible violation of any law or
              regulation but whose reporting is not required by this section.


              (2) A transaction requires reporting under the terms of this section
              if it is conducted or attempted by, at, or through a money services
              business, involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $2,000
              (except as provided in paragraph (a)(3) of this section), and the money
              services business knows, suspects, or has reason to suspect that the
              transaction (or a pattern of transactions of which the transaction is
              a part):


              (i) Involves funds derived from illegal activity or is intended or conducted
              in order to hide or disguise funds or assets derived from illegal activity
              (including, without limitation, the ownership, nature, source, location,
              or control of such funds or assets) as part of a plan to violate or
              evade any Federal law or regulation or to avoid any transaction reporting
              requirement under Federal law or regulation;


              (ii) Is designed, whether through structuring or other means, to evade
              any requirements of this chapter or of any other regulations promulgated
              under the Bank Secrecy Act ; or


              (iii) Serves no business or apparent lawful purpose, and the reporting
              money services business knows of no reasonable explanation for the transaction
              after examining the available facts, including the background and possible
              purpose of the transaction .


              (iv) Involves use of the money services business to facilitate criminal
              activity.


              (3) To the extent that the identification of transactions required to
              be reported is derived from a review of clearance records or other similar
              records of money orders or traveler''s checks that have been sold or
              processed, an issuer of money orders or traveler''s checks shall only
              be required to report a transaction or pattern of transactions that
              involves or aggregates funds or other assets of at least $5,000.


              (4) The obligation to identify and properly and timely to report a suspicious
              transaction rests with each money services business involved in the
              transaction , provided that no more than one report is required to be
              filed by the money services businesses involved in a particular transaction
              (so long as the report filed contains all relevant facts). Whether,
              in addition to any liability on its own for failure to report, a money
              services business that issues the instrument or provides the funds transfer
              service involved in the transaction may be liable for the failure of
              another money services business involved in the transaction to report
              that transaction depends upon the nature of the contractual or other
              relationship between the businesses, and the legal effect of the facts
              and circumstances of the relationship and transaction involved, under
              general principles of the law of agency.


              (b)  Filing procedures —(1) What to file. A suspicious transaction shall
              be reported by completing a Suspicious Activity Report(“SAR”), and collecting
              and maintaining supporting documentation as required by paragraph (c)
              of this section.


              (2)  Where to file. The SAR shall be filed in a central location to
              be determined by FinCEN , as indicated in the instructions to the SAR.'

          available_excerpt: '# Research this issue for the dossier: State money-transmission
            authority after


            ## Question


            Research this issue for the dossier: State money-transmission authority
            after the asset purchase. The migration plan assumes Harbor’s licenses
            will permit service in four new markets. An asset purchase might not transfer
            those licenses or their operating authority to Mosaic.

            Lawyer''s accepted priorities (private matter context):

            Confirm that Mosaic can lawfully serve each migrated account and market.:
            The asset-purchase structure and incomplete identity records undermine
            the planned day-one launch.

            Proposed date leads; resolve each reference in saved records before using
            it: [{"role": "target acquisition close", "value": "Nov 15", "source_reference_id":
            "MSG-20260909-982278", "note": "The source describes this date as about
            ten weeks away. It does not expressly state the year or timezone."}, {"role":
            "identity-verification vendor closure", "value": "2024", "source_reference_id":
            "MSG-20260909-982278", "note": "The record reports that Harbor\u2019s
            identity-verification vendor closed in 2024."}]

        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000014
            start: 0
            end: 1963
            page_number: 14
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 5b5d2f6c388b9bcf98252d205bb001dbdce26951229e0e6896dfecf0358be545
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000014.md
            text: "14 \n \nARTICLE V \nMoney Transmission Licenses \nSection 5.01
              License Required. \n(a) \nA person may not engage in the business of
              money transmission or \nadvertise, solicit, or hold itself out as providing
              money transmission unless the person is licensed \nunder this [Act];
              \n(b) \n[Section 5.01(a)] does not apply to: \n(1) \nA person that is
              an authorized delegate of a person licensed under \nthis [Act] acting
              within the scope of authority conferred by a written contract with the
              \nlicensee; or \n(2) \nA person that is exempt pursuant to [Section
              3.01] and does not \nengage in money transmission outside the scope
              of such exemption. \n(c) \nA license issued under [Section 5.05] is
              not transferable or assignable. \nSection 5.02 Consistent State Licensing.
              \n(a) \nTo establish consistent licensing between [this state] and other
              states, the \n[Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged to: \n(1)
              \nimplement all licensing provisions of this [Act] in a manner that
              is \nconsistent with other states that have adopted this [Act]or multistate
              licensing processes; \nand \n(2) \nparticipate in nationwide protocols
              for licensing cooperation and \ncoordination among state regulators
              provided that such protocols are consistent with this \n[Act]. \n(b)
              \nIn order to fulfill the purposes of this [Act], the [Commissioner]
              is \nauthorized and encouraged to establish relationships or contracts
              with NMLS or other entities \ndesignated by NMLS to enable the [Commissioner]
              to: \n(1) \ncollect and maintain records; \n(2) \ncoordinate multistate
              licensing processes and supervision processes; \n(3) \nprocess fees;
              and \n(4) \nfacilitate communication between [state] and licensees or
              other \npersons subject to this [Act]. \n(c) \nThe [Commissioner] is
              authorized and encouraged to utilize NMLS for all \naspects of licensing
              in accordance with this [Act], including but not limited to, license
              applications, \napplications for acquisitions of control, surety bonds,
              reporting, criminal history background\n"
          support_state: retrieved
          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/RUN-20260911-d08e84-37ee676da5b354ba8538-fetch.md.bin
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000014.md
          source_hash: 5b5d2f6c388b9bcf98252d205bb001dbdce26951229e0e6896dfecf0358be545
          source_label: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act — Page 14,
            characters 0–1963'
          unit_id: p000014
          locator: Page 14, characters 0–1963
          available_excerpt: "14 \n \nARTICLE V \nMoney Transmission Licenses \nSection
            5.01 License Required. \n(a) \nA person may not engage in the business
            of money transmission or \nadvertise, solicit, or hold itself out as providing
            money transmission unless the person is licensed \nunder this [Act]; \n(b)
            \n[Section 5.01(a)] does not apply to: \n(1) \nA person that is an authorized
            delegate of a person licensed under \nthis [Act] acting within the scope
            of authority conferred by a written contract with the \nlicensee; or \n(2)
            \nA person that is exempt pursuant to [Section 3.01] and does not \nengage
            in money transmission outside the scope of such exemption. \n(c) \nA license
            issued under [Section 5.05] is not transferable or assignable. \nSection
            5.02 Consistent State Licensing. \n(a) \nTo establish consistent licensing
            between [this state] and other states, the \n[Commissioner] is authorized
            and encouraged to: \n(1) \nimplement all licensing provisions of this
            [Act] in a manner that is \nconsistent with other states that have adopted
            this [Act]or multistate licensing processes; \nand \n(2) \nparticipate
            in nationwide protocols for licensing cooperation and \ncoordination among
            state regulators provided that such protocols are consistent with this
            \n[Act]. \n(b) \nIn order to fulfill the purposes of this [Act], the [Commissioner]
            is \nauthorized and encouraged to establish relationships or contracts
            with NMLS or other entities \ndesignated by NMLS to enable the [Commissioner]
            to: \n(1) \ncollect and maintain records; \n(2) \ncoordinate multistate
            licensing processes and supervision processes; \n(3) \nprocess fees; and
            \n(4) \nfacilitate communication between [state] and licensees or other
            \npersons subject to this [Act]. \n(c) \nThe [Commissioner] is authorized
            and encouraged to utilize NMLS for all \naspects of licensing in accordance
            with this [Act], including but not limited to, license applications, \napplications
            for acquisitions of control, surety bonds, reporting, criminal history
            background\n"
        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000022
            start: 0
            end: 2612
            page_number: 22
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 175b57e812c71a8532569030d2db7113b5adc94e61bd9922dc0fee5d833c2748
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000022.md
            text: "22 \n \n(e) \nWhen an application for acquisition of control under
              this [Section 6.01] \nappears to include all the items and address all
              of the matters that are required, the application shall \nbe considered
              complete and the [Commissioner] shall promptly notify the applicant
              in a record of \nthe date on which the application was determined to
              be complete and:15 \n \n(1) \nThe [Commissioner] shall approve or deny
              the application within 60 \ndays after the completion date; or \n(2)
              \nif the application is not approved or denied within 60 days after
              the \ncompletion date: \n(A) \nthe application is approved; and \n(B)
              \nthe person, or group of persons acting in concert, are not \nprohibited
              from acquiring control. \n(3) \nThe [Commissioner] may for good cause
              extend the application \nperiod. \n(f) \nA determination by the [Commissioner]
              that an application is complete and \nis accepted for processing means
              only that the application, on its face, appears to include all of the
              \nitems and address all of the matters that are required, and is not
              an assessment of the substance of \nthe application or of the sufficiency
              of the information provided. \n(g) \nWhen an application is filed and
              considered complete under [Section \n6.01(e)], the [Commissioner] shall
              investigate the financial condition and responsibility, financial \nand
              business experience, character, and general fitness of the person, or
              group of persons acting in \nconcert, seeking to acquire control. The
              [Commissioner] shall approve an acquisition of control \npursuant to
              this [Section 6.01] if the [Commissioner] finds that all of the following
              conditions have \nbeen fulfilled: \n(1) \nThe requirements of subsections
              (b) and (d) have been met, as \napplicable; and \n(2) \nthe financial
              condition and responsibility, financial and business \nexperience, competence,
              character, and general fitness of the person, or group of persons \nacting
              in concert, seeking to acquire control; and the competence, experience,
              character, \nand general fitness of the key individuals and persons
              that would be in control of the \nlicensee after the acquisition of
              control indicate that it is in the interest of the public to \npermit
              the person, or group of persons acting in concert, to control the licensee.
              \n(h) \nIf an applicant avails itself or is otherwise subject to a multistate
              licensing \n \n15 Commentary: NMLS will accommodate and streamline the
              process of communicating when applications are \nreceived and when states
              consider them to have been complete. The intent of this Act is for the
              application period to \nbegin on the day an application is determined
              to be complete.\n"
          support_state: retrieved
          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/RUN-20260911-d08e84-37ee676da5b354ba8538-fetch.md.bin
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000022.md
          source_hash: 175b57e812c71a8532569030d2db7113b5adc94e61bd9922dc0fee5d833c2748
          source_label: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act — Page 22,
            characters 0–2612'
          unit_id: p000022
          locator: Page 22, characters 0–2612
          available_excerpt: "22 \n \n(e) \nWhen an application for acquisition of
            control under this [Section 6.01] \nappears to include all the items and
            address all of the matters that are required, the application shall \nbe
            considered complete and the [Commissioner] shall promptly notify the applicant
            in a record of \nthe date on which the application was determined to be
            complete and:15 \n \n(1) \nThe [Commissioner] shall approve or deny the
            application within 60 \ndays after the completion date; or \n(2) \nif
            the application is not approved or denied within 60 days after the \ncompletion
            date: \n(A) \nthe application is approved; and \n(B) \nthe person, or
            group of persons acting in concert, are not \nprohibited from acquiring
            control. \n(3) \nThe [Commissioner] may for good cause extend the application
            \nperiod. \n(f) \nA determination by the [Commissioner] that an application
            is complete and \nis accepted for processing means only that the application,
            on its face, appears to include all of the \nitems and address all of
            the matters that are required, and is not an assessment of the substance
            of \nthe application or of the sufficiency of the information provided.
            \n(g) \nWhen an application is filed and considered complete under [Section
            \n6.01(e)], the [Commissioner] shall investigate the financial condition
            and responsibility, financial \nand business experience, character, and
            general fitness of the person, or group of persons acting in \nconcert,
            seeking to acquire control. The [Commissioner] shall approve an acquisition
            of control \npursuant to this [Section 6.01] if the [Commissioner] finds
            that all of the following conditions have \nbeen fulfilled: \n(1) \nThe
            requirements of subsections (b) and (d) have been met, as \napplicable;
            and \n(2) \nthe financial condition and responsibility, financial and
            business \nexperience, competence, character, and general fitness of the
            person, or group of persons \nacting in concert, seeking to acquire control;
            and the competence, experience, character, \nand general fitness of the
            key individuals and persons that would be in control of the \nlicensee
            after the acquisition of control indicate that it is in the interest of
            the public to \npermit the person, or group of persons acting in concert,
            to control the licensee. \n(h) \nIf an applicant avails itself or is otherwise
            subject to a multistate licensing \n \n15 Commentary: NMLS will accommodate
            and streamline the process of communicating when applications are \nreceived
            and when states consider them to have been complete. The intent of this
            Act is for the application period to \nbegin on the day an application
            is determined to be complete.\n"
        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000023
            start: 0
            end: 2523
            page_number: 23
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 83a664c778fbc9de0b4d13270e2a918f6488b461db1a6617e3d36032163068ca
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000023.md
            text: "23 \n \nprocess: \n(1) \nthe [Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged
              to accept the \ninvestigation results of a lead investigative state
              for the purpose of [Section 6.01(g)] if the \nlead investigative state
              has sufficient staffing, expertise, and minimum standards; or \n(2)
              \nif [state] is a lead investigative state, the [Commissioner] is \nauthorized
              and encouraged to investigate the applicant pursuant to [Section 6.01(g)]
              and \nthe timeframes established by agreement through the multistate
              licensing process. \n(i) \nThe [Commissioner] shall issue a formal written
              notice of the denial of an \napplication to acquire control within thirty
              (30) days of the decision to deny the application. The \n[Commissioner]
              shall set forth in the notice of denial the specific reasons for the
              denial of the \napplication. An applicant whose application is denied
              by the [Commissioner] under this [Section \n6.01(i)] may appeal within
              thirty (30) days16 after receipt of the written notice of the denial
              \n[pursuant to state administrative law procedures]. \n(j) \nThe requirements
              of [Section 6.01(a) and (b)] do not apply to any of the \nfollowing:
              \n(1) \nA person that acts as a proxy for the sole purpose of voting
              at a \ndesignated meeting of the shareholders or holders of voting shares
              or voting interests of a \nlicensee or a person in control of a licensee;
              \n(2) \nA person that acquires control of a licensee by devise or descent;
              \n(3) \nA person that acquires control of a licensee as a personal \nrepresentative,
              custodian, guardian, conservator, or trustee, or as an officer appointed
              by a \ncourt of competent jurisdiction or by operation of law; \n(4)
              \nA person that is exempt under [Section 3.01(g)]; \n(5) \nA person
              that the [Commissioner] determines is not subject to \n[Section 6.01(a)]
              based on the public interest; \n(6) \nA public offering of securities
              of a licensee or a person in control of \na licensee; or \n(7) \nAn
              internal reorganization of a person in control of the licensee \nwhere
              the ultimate person in control of the licensee remains the same. \n(k)
              \nPersons in [Sections 6.01(j)(2), (3), (4), (6), (7)] in cooperation
              with the \nlicensee shall notify the [Commissioner] within [15] days
              after the acquisition of control.17 \n \n16 Commentary: Administrative
              procedures for challenging a departmental decision may be derived from
              other \naspects of state law. In such a case, this Act should be adjusted
              to reflect the relevant administrative process. \n17 Commentary: Notice
              is satisfied upon updating the licensee’s NMLS record.\n"
          support_state: retrieved
          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/RUN-20260911-d08e84-37ee676da5b354ba8538-fetch.md.bin

          unit_id: p000023
          locator: Page 23, characters 0–2523
          available_excerpt: "23 \n \nprocess: \n(1) \nthe [Commissioner] is authorized
            and encouraged to accept the \ninvestigation results of a lead investigative
            state for the purpose of [Section 6.01(g)] if the \nlead investigative
            state has sufficient staffing, expertise, and minimum standards; or \n(2)
            \nif [state] is a lead investigative state, the [Commissioner] is \nauthorized
            and encouraged to investigate the applicant pursuant to [Section 6.01(g)]
            and \nthe timeframes established by agreement through the multistate licensing
            process. \n(i) \nThe [Commissioner] shall issue a formal written notice
            of the denial of an \napplication to acquire control within thirty (30)
            days of the decision to deny the application. The \n[Commissioner] shall
            set forth in the notice of denial the specific reasons for the denial
            of the \napplication. An applicant whose application is denied by the
            [Commissioner] under this [Section \n6.01(i)] may appeal within thirty
            (30) days16 after receipt of the written notice of the denial \n[pursuant
            to state administrative law procedures]. \n(j) \nThe requirements of [Section
            6.01(a) and (b)] do not apply to any of the \nfollowing: \n(1) \nA person
            that acts as a proxy for the sole purpose of voting at a \ndesignated
            meeting of the shareholders or holders of voting shares or voting interests
            of a \nlicensee or a person in control of a licensee; \n(2) \nA person
            that acquires control of a licensee by devise or descent; \n(3) \nA person
            that acquires control of a licensee as a personal \nrepresentative, custodian,
            guardian, conservator, or trustee, or as an officer appointed by a \ncourt
            of competent jurisdiction or by operation of law; \n(4) \nA person that
            is exempt under [Section 3.01(g)]; \n(5) \nA person that the [Commissioner]
            determines is not subject to \n[Section 6.01(a)] based on the public interest;
            \n(6) \nA public offering of securities of a licensee or a person in control
            of \na licensee; or \n(7) \nAn internal reorganization of a person in
            control of the licensee \nwhere the ultimate person in control of the
            licensee remains the same. \n(k) \nPersons in [Sections 6.01(j)(2), (3),
            (4), (6), (7)] in cooperation with the \nlicensee shall notify the [Commissioner]
            within [15] days after the acquisition of control.17 \n \n16 Commentary:
            Administrative procedures for challenging a departmental decision may
            be derived from other \naspects of state law. In such a case, this Act
            should be adjusted to reflect the relevant administrative process. \n17
            Commentary: Notice is satisfied upon updating the licensee’s NMLS record.\n"
        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000024
            start: 0
            end: 2799
            page_number: 24
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 4732d9c2b053975a4d3cb27db8e99faa4baf45f71eb350fa519e31b5890ac680
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000024.md
            text: "24 \n \n(l) \nStreamlined Acquisition of Control \n(1) \nThe requirements
              of [Section 6.01(a) and (b)] do not apply to a \nperson that has complied
              with and received approval to engage in money transmission \nunder this
              [Act] or was identified as a person in control in a prior application
              filed with and \napproved by the [Commissioner] or by an MSB accredited
              state pursuant to a multistate \nlicensing process, provided that: \n(A)
              \nthe person has not had a license revoked or suspended or \ncontrolled
              a licensee that has had a license revoked or suspended while the person
              \nwas in control of the licensee in the previous 5-years; \n(B) \nif
              the person is a licensee, the person is well managed and \nhas received
              at least a satisfactory rating for compliance at its most recent \nexamination
              by an MSB accredited state if such rating was given; \n(C) \nthe licensee
              to be acquired is projected to meet the \nrequirements of [Sections
              10.01, 10.02, and 10.03] of this [Act] after the acquisition \nof control
              is completed, and if the person acquiring control is a licensee, that
              \nlicensee is also projected to meet the requirements of [Sections 10.01,
              10.02, and \n10.03] of this [Act] after the acquisition of control is
              completed; \n(D) \nthe licensee to be acquired will not implement any
              material \nchanges to its business plan as a result of the acquisition
              of control, and if the person \nacquiring control is a licensee, that
              licensee also will not implement any material \nchanges to its business
              plan as a result of the acquisition of control;  and \n(E) \nthe person
              provides notice of the acquisition in cooperation \nwith the licensee
              and attests to [Section 6.01(l)(1)(A), (B), (C), and (D)] in a form
              \nand in a medium prescribed by the [Commissioner]. \n(2) \nIf the notice
              is not disapproved within 30 days after the date on \nwhich the notice
              was determined to be complete, the notice is deemed approved. \n(m)
              \nBefore filing an application for approval to acquire control of a
              licensee a \nperson may request in writing a determination from the
              [Commissioner] as to whether the person \nwould be considered a person
              in control of a licensee upon consummation of a proposed \ntransaction.
              If the [Commissioner] determines that the person would not be a person
              in control of \na licensee, the proposed Person and transaction is not
              subject to the requirements of [Section \n6.01(a) and (b)] of this [Act].
              \n(n) \nIf a multistate licensing process includes a determination pursuant
              to \n[Section 6.01(m)] and an applicant avails itself or is otherwise
              subject to the multistate licensing \nprocess: \n(1) \nThe [Commissioner]
              is authorized and encouraged to accept the \ncontrol determination of
              a lead investigative state with sufficient staffing, expertise, and
              \nminimum standards for the purpose of [Section 6.01(m)]; or\n"
          support_state: retrieved
          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf
