          source_hash: 4732d9c2b053975a4d3cb27db8e99faa4baf45f71eb350fa519e31b5890ac680
          source_label: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act — Page 24,
            characters 0–2799'
          unit_id: p000024
          locator: Page 24, characters 0–2799
          available_excerpt: "24 \n \n(l) \nStreamlined Acquisition of Control \n(1)
            \nThe requirements of [Section 6.01(a) and (b)] do not apply to a \nperson
            that has complied with and received approval to engage in money transmission
            \nunder this [Act] or was identified as a person in control in a prior
            application filed with and \napproved by the [Commissioner] or by an MSB
            accredited state pursuant to a multistate \nlicensing process, provided
            that: \n(A) \nthe person has not had a license revoked or suspended or
            \ncontrolled a licensee that has had a license revoked or suspended while
            the person \nwas in control of the licensee in the previous 5-years; \n(B)
            \nif the person is a licensee, the person is well managed and \nhas received
            at least a satisfactory rating for compliance at its most recent \nexamination
            by an MSB accredited state if such rating was given; \n(C) \nthe licensee
            to be acquired is projected to meet the \nrequirements of [Sections 10.01,
            10.02, and 10.03] of this [Act] after the acquisition \nof control is
            completed, and if the person acquiring control is a licensee, that \nlicensee
            is also projected to meet the requirements of [Sections 10.01, 10.02,
            and \n10.03] of this [Act] after the acquisition of control is completed;
            \n(D) \nthe licensee to be acquired will not implement any material \nchanges
            to its business plan as a result of the acquisition of control, and if
            the person \nacquiring control is a licensee, that licensee also will
            not implement any material \nchanges to its business plan as a result
            of the acquisition of control;  and \n(E) \nthe person provides notice
            of the acquisition in cooperation \nwith the licensee and attests to [Section
            6.01(l)(1)(A), (B), (C), and (D)] in a form \nand in a medium prescribed
            by the [Commissioner]. \n(2) \nIf the notice is not disapproved within
            30 days after the date on \nwhich the notice was determined to be complete,
            the notice is deemed approved. \n(m) \nBefore filing an application for
            approval to acquire control of a licensee a \nperson may request in writing
            a determination from the [Commissioner] as to whether the person \nwould
            be considered a person in control of a licensee upon consummation of a
            proposed \ntransaction. If the [Commissioner] determines that the person
            would not be a person in control of \na licensee, the proposed Person
            and transaction is not subject to the requirements of [Section \n6.01(a)
            and (b)] of this [Act]. \n(n) \nIf a multistate licensing process includes
            a determination pursuant to \n[Section 6.01(m)] and an applicant avails
            itself or is otherwise subject to the multistate licensing \nprocess:
            \n(1) \nThe [Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged to accept the
            \ncontrol determination of a lead investigative state with sufficient
            staffing, expertise, and \nminimum standards for the purpose of [Section
            6.01(m)]; or\n"
        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000015
            start: 0
            end: 2835
            page_number: 15
            section_label: null
            body_hash: bba81d751ac954c1c9099842578104bf494b4912b32ce1f2e32cc8fac953e9e7
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000015.md
            text: "15 \n \nchecks, credit checks, fee processing, and examinations.9
              \n(d) \nThe [Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged to utilize NMLS
              forms, \nprocesses, and functionalities in accordance with this [Act].10
              In the event NMLS does not provide \nfunctionality, forms, or processes
              for a provision of this [Act], the [Commissioner] is authorized \nand
              encouraged to strive to implement the requirements in a manner that
              facilitates uniformity \nwith respect to licensing, supervision, reporting,
              and regulation of licensees which are licensed in \nmultiple jurisdictions.
              \n(e) \nWaive or Modify Requirements.— For the purpose of participating
              in the \nNationwide Multistate Licensing System & Registry, the Commissioner
              is authorized to waive or \nmodify, in whole or in part, by rule, regulation
              or order, any or all of the requirements and to \nestablish new requirements
              as reasonably necessary to participate in the Nationwide Multistate
              \nLicensing System & Registry. \n[Alternative to Section 5.02(e)]: The
              [Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged to establish \nand adopt,
              by rule or regulation, requirements for participation by applicants
              and licensees in \nNMLS upon the [Department's] determination that each
              requirement is consistent with law, public \ninterest, and the purposes
              of this [Section 5.02].] \nSection 5.03 Application for License. \n(a)
              \nApplicants for a license shall apply in a form and in a medium as
              prescribed \nby the [Commissioner]. Each such form shall contain content
              as set forth by rule, regulation, \ninstruction or procedure of the
              [Commissioner] and may be changed or updated by the \n[Commissioner]
              in accordance with applicable law in order to carry out the purposes
              of this [Act] \nand maintain consistency with NMLS licensing standards
              and practices. The application must state \nor contain, as applicable:
              \n(1) \nthe legal name and residential and business addresses of the
              \napplicant and any fictitious or trade name used by the applicant in
              conducting its business; \n(2) \na list of any criminal convictions
              of the applicant and any material \nlitigation in which the applicant
              has been involved in the [10]-year period next preceding \nthe submission
              of the application; \n(3) \na description of any money transmission
              previously provided by the \napplicant and the money transmission that
              the applicant seeks to provide in this state; \n \n9 Commentary: The
              [Commissioner] acting through its representatives should review and
              ensure that NMLS \nfunctionalities, forms, and processes are consistent
              with this Act before adopting such NMLS functionalities, forms, \nand
              processes. \n10 Commentary: The [Commissioner] acting through its representatives
              should review and ensure that NMLS \nfunctionalities, forms, and processes
              are consistent with this Act before adopting such NMLS functionalities,
              forms, \nand processes.\n"
          support_state: retrieved
          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/RUN-20260911-d08e84-37ee676da5b354ba8538-fetch.md.bin
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000015.md
          source_hash: bba81d751ac954c1c9099842578104bf494b4912b32ce1f2e32cc8fac953e9e7
          source_label: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act — Page 15,
            characters 0–2835'
          unit_id: p000015
          locator: Page 15, characters 0–2835
          available_excerpt: "15 \n \nchecks, credit checks, fee processing, and examinations.9
            \n(d) \nThe [Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged to utilize NMLS
            forms, \nprocesses, and functionalities in accordance with this [Act].10
            In the event NMLS does not provide \nfunctionality, forms, or processes
            for a provision of this [Act], the [Commissioner] is authorized \nand
            encouraged to strive to implement the requirements in a manner that facilitates
            uniformity \nwith respect to licensing, supervision, reporting, and regulation
            of licensees which are licensed in \nmultiple jurisdictions. \n(e) \nWaive
            or Modify Requirements.— For the purpose of participating in the \nNationwide
            Multistate Licensing System & Registry, the Commissioner is authorized
            to waive or \nmodify, in whole or in part, by rule, regulation or order,
            any or all of the requirements and to \nestablish new requirements as
            reasonably necessary to participate in the Nationwide Multistate \nLicensing
            System & Registry. \n[Alternative to Section 5.02(e)]: The [Commissioner]
            is authorized and encouraged to establish \nand adopt, by rule or regulation,
            requirements for participation by applicants and licensees in \nNMLS upon
            the [Department's] determination that each requirement is consistent with
            law, public \ninterest, and the purposes of this [Section 5.02].] \nSection
            5.03 Application for License. \n(a) \nApplicants for a license shall apply
            in a form and in a medium as prescribed \nby the [Commissioner]. Each
            such form shall contain content as set forth by rule, regulation, \ninstruction
            or procedure of the [Commissioner] and may be changed or updated by the
            \n[Commissioner] in accordance with applicable law in order to carry out
            the purposes of this [Act] \nand maintain consistency with NMLS licensing
            standards and practices. The application must state \nor contain, as applicable:
            \n(1) \nthe legal name and residential and business addresses of the \napplicant
            and any fictitious or trade name used by the applicant in conducting its
            business; \n(2) \na list of any criminal convictions of the applicant
            and any material \nlitigation in which the applicant has been involved
            in the [10]-year period next preceding \nthe submission of the application;
            \n(3) \na description of any money transmission previously provided by
            the \napplicant and the money transmission that the applicant seeks to
            provide in this state; \n \n9 Commentary: The [Commissioner] acting through
            its representatives should review and ensure that NMLS \nfunctionalities,
            forms, and processes are consistent with this Act before adopting such
            NMLS functionalities, forms, \nand processes. \n10 Commentary: The [Commissioner]
            acting through its representatives should review and ensure that NMLS
            \nfunctionalities, forms, and processes are consistent with this Act before
            adopting such NMLS functionalities, forms, \nand processes.\n"
        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000021
            start: 0
            end: 2389
            page_number: 21
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 5638ae03ab2c6c110a80e30639b8ccd42a1c585fdc2818a38e4f2f0fc69a5e06
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000021.md
            text: "21 \n \nin a medium prescribed by the [Commissioner]. The renewal
              report must state or contain a \ndescription of each material change
              in information submitted by the licensee in its original license \napplication
              which has not been reported to the [Commissioner]. \n(c) \nThe [Commissioner]
              for good cause may grant an extension of the renewal \ndate. \n(d) \nThe
              [Commissioner] is authorized and encouraged to utilize NMLS to \nprocess
              license renewals provided that such functionality is consistent with
              this [Section 5.06]. \nSection 5.07 Maintenance of License. \n(a) \nIf
              a licensee does not continue to meet the qualifications or satisfy the
              \nrequirements that apply to an applicant for a new money transmission
              license, the [Commissioner] \nmay suspend or revoke the licensee's license
              in accordance with the procedures established by this \n[Act] or other
              applicable state law for such suspension or revocation. \n(b) \nAn applicant
              for a money transmission license must demonstrate that it \nmeets or
              will meet, and a money transmission licensee must at all times meet,
              the requirements in \n[Sections 10.01, 10.02, and 10.03] of this [Act].
              \nARTICLE VI \nAcquisition of Control and Change of Key Individual \nSection
              6.01 Acquisition of Control. \n(a) \nAny person, or group of persons
              acting in concert, seeking to acquire control \nof a licensee shall
              obtain the written approval of the [Commissioner] prior to acquiring
              control. \nAn individual is not deemed to acquire control of a licensee
              and is not subject to these acquisition \nof control provisions when
              that individual becomes a key individual in the ordinary course of \nbusiness.
              \n(b) \nA person, or group of persons acting in concert, seeking to
              acquire control \nof a licensee shall, in cooperation with the licensee:
              \n(1) \nSubmit an application in a form and in a medium prescribed by
              the \n[Commissioner]; and \n(2) \nSubmit a nonrefundable fee of [$X]
              with the request for approval. \n(c) \nUpon request, the [Commissioner]
              may permit a licensee or the person, or \ngroup of persons acting in
              concert, to submit some or all information required by the \n[Commissioner]
              pursuant to [Section 6.01(b)(1)] without using NMLS. \n(d) \nThe application
              required by [Section 6.01(b)(1)] shall include information \nrequired
              by [Section 5.04] for any new key individuals that have not previously
              completed the \nrequirements of [Section 5.04] for a licensee.\n"

          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf

            \n(a) \nIf a licensee does not continue to meet the qualifications or

            it \nmeets or will meet, and a money transmission licensee must at all

            \nSection 6.01 Acquisition of Control. \n(a) \nAny person, or group of
            persons acting in concert, seeking to acquire control \nof a licensee
            shall obtain the written approval of the [Commissioner] prior to acquiring

            \n(b) \nA person, or group of persons acting in concert, seeking to acquire

            and \n(2) \nSubmit a nonrefundable fee of [$X] with the request for approval.
            \n(c) \nUpon request, the [Commissioner] may permit a licensee or the
            person, or \ngroup of persons acting in concert, to submit some or all

        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000030
            start: 0
            end: 2670
            page_number: 30
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 0d032292dd6b007bb0772b41f7c3f21512a56d4557c8c7b69696774b6248c842
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000030.md
            text: "30 \n \n(1) \nappoint the person signing the contract as the licensee's
              authorized \ndelegate with the authority to conduct money transmission
              on behalf of the licensee; \n(2) \nset forth the nature and scope of
              the relationship between the \nlicensee and the authorized delegate
              and the respective rights and responsibilities of the \nparties; \n(3)
              \nrequire the authorized delegate to agree to fully comply with all
              \napplicable state and federal laws, rules, and regulations pertaining
              to money transmission, \nincluding this [Act] and regulations implementing
              this [Act], relevant provisions of the \nBank Secrecy Act and the USA
              PATRIOT ACT; \n(4) \nrequire the authorized delegate to remit and handle
              money and \nmonetary value in accordance with the terms of the contract
              between the licensee and the \nauthorized delegate; \n(5) \nimpose a
              trust on money and monetary value net of fees received for \nmoney transmission
              for the benefit of the licensee; \n(6) \nrequire the authorized delegate
              to prepare and maintain records as \nrequired by this [Act] or regulations
              implementing this [Act], or as reasonably requested \nby the [Commissioner];
              \n(7) \nacknowledge that the authorized delegate consents to examination
              \nor investigation by the [Commissioner]; \n(8) \nstate that the licensee
              is subject to regulation by the [Commissioner] \nand that, as part of
              that regulation, the [Commissioner] may suspend or revoke an \nauthorized
              delegate designation or require the licensee to terminate an authorized
              delegate \ndesignation; and \n(9) \n(9)  acknowledge receipt of the
              written policies and procedures \nrequired under [Section 8.01(b)(1)].
              \n(e) \nIf the licensee's license is suspended, revoked, surrendered,
              or expired, the \nlicensee must, within five (5) business days, provide
              documentation to the [Commissioner] that \nthe licensee has notified
              all applicable authorized delegates of the licensee whose names are
              in a \nrecord filed with the [Commissioner] of the suspension, revocation,
              surrender, or expiration of a \nlicense. Upon suspension, revocation,
              surrender, or expiration of a license, applicable authorized \ndelegates
              shall immediately cease to provide money transmission as an authorized
              delegate of the \nlicensee. \n(f) \nAn authorized delegate of a licensee
              holds in trust for the benefit of the \nlicensee all money net of fees
              received from money transmission.  If any authorized delegate \ncommingles
              any funds received from money transmission with any other funds or property
              owned \nor controlled by the authorized delegate, all commingled funds
              and other property shall be \nconsidered held in trust in favor of the
              licensee in an amount equal to the amount of money net of\n"

          url: https://www.csbs.org/sites/default/files/2023-02/CSBS%20Money%20Transmission%20Modernization%20Act.pdf

          available_excerpt: "30 \n \n(1) \nappoint the person signing the contract
            as the licensee's authorized \ndelegate with the authority to conduct
            money transmission on behalf of the licensee; \n(2) \nset forth the nature

            \n(3) \nrequire the authorized delegate to agree to fully comply with
            all \napplicable state and federal laws, rules, and regulations pertaining
            to money transmission, \nincluding this [Act] and regulations implementing
            this [Act], relevant provisions of the \nBank Secrecy Act and the USA
            PATRIOT ACT; \n(4) \nrequire the authorized delegate to remit and handle
            money and \nmonetary value in accordance with the terms of the contract
            between the licensee and the \nauthorized delegate; \n(5) \nimpose a trust
            on money and monetary value net of fees received for \nmoney transmission
            for the benefit of the licensee; \n(6) \nrequire the authorized delegate
            to prepare and maintain records as \nrequired by this [Act] or regulations
            implementing this [Act], or as reasonably requested \nby the [Commissioner];
            \n(7) \nacknowledge that the authorized delegate consents to examination
            \nor investigation by the [Commissioner]; \n(8) \nstate that the licensee
            is subject to regulation by the [Commissioner] \nand that, as part of
            that regulation, the [Commissioner] may suspend or revoke an \nauthorized
            delegate designation or require the licensee to terminate an authorized
            delegate \ndesignation; and \n(9) \n(9)  acknowledge receipt of the written
            policies and procedures \nrequired under [Section 8.01(b)(1)]. \n(e) \nIf
            the licensee's license is suspended, revoked, surrendered, or expired,
            the \nlicensee must, within five (5) business days, provide documentation
            to the [Commissioner] that \nthe licensee has notified all applicable
            authorized delegates of the licensee whose names are in a \nrecord filed
            with the [Commissioner] of the suspension, revocation, surrender, or expiration
            of a \nlicense. Upon suspension, revocation, surrender, or expiration
            of a license, applicable authorized \ndelegates shall immediately cease
            to provide money transmission as an authorized delegate of the \nlicensee.
            \n(f) \nAn authorized delegate of a licensee holds in trust for the benefit
            of the \nlicensee all money net of fees received from money transmission.
            \ If any authorized delegate \ncommingles any funds received from money
            transmission with any other funds or property owned \nor controlled by
            the authorized delegate, all commingled funds and other property shall
            be \nconsidered held in trust in favor of the licensee in an amount equal
            to the amount of money net of\n"
        - source_id: SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538
          source_version: dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a
          title: '[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act'
          extraction_state: complete
          selected_passages:
          - unit_id: p000031
            start: 0
            end: 2704
            page_number: 31
            section_label: null
            body_hash: 994d5382d8c2640923eca8c8aabbca98c8fbaeec037edafbf7675c1a3819b1e7
            path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538/dd8e38dc121ab8ed7fe079b02c7e158a/pages/p000031.md
            text: "31 \n \nfees received from money transmission.22 \n(g) \nAn authorized
              delegate may not use a subdelegate to conduct money \ntransmission on
              behalf of a licensee. \nSection 8.02 Unauthorized Activities \nA person
              shall not engage in the business of money transmission on behalf of
              a person \nnot licensed under this [Act]  or not exempt pursuant to
              [Article III] of this [Act]. A person that \nengages in such activity
              provides money transmission to the same extent as if the person were
              a \nlicensee, and shall be jointly and severally liable with the unlicensed
              or nonexempt person. \nSection 8.03 [Optional]: Prohibited Authorized
              Delegates. \n(a) \nThe [appropriate court] in an action brought by a
              licensee shall have \njurisdiction to grant appropriate equitable or
              legal relief, including without limitation prohibiting \nthe authorized
              delegate from directly or indirectly acting as an authorized delegate
              for any licensee \nin this state and the payment of restitution, damages
              or other monetary relief, if the [appropriate \ncourt] finds that an
              authorized delegate failed to remit money in accordance with the written
              \ncontract required by [Section 8.01(b)] or as otherwise directed by
              the licensee or required by law. \n(b) \nIf the [appropriate court]
              issues an order prohibiting a person from acting as \nan authorized
              delegate for any licensee pursuant to [Section 8.03(a)], the licensee
              that brought the \naction shall report the order to the [Commissioner]
              within thirty (30) days and shall report the \norder through NMLS within
              ninety (90) days. \n(c) \nAn authorized delegate who holds money in
              trust for the benefit of a \nlicensee and knowingly fails to remit more
              than [$XXX] of such money is guilty of a [reference \nto state classification]
              felony. \n(d) \nAn authorized delegate who holds money in trust for
              the benefit of a \nlicensee and knowingly fails to remit no more than
              [$XXX] of such money is guilty of a [reference \nto state classification]
              misdemeanor. \nARTICLE IX  \nTimely Transmission, Refunds, and Disclosures
              \nSection 9.01 Timely Transmission. \n(a) \nEvery licensee shall forward
              all money received for transmission in \naccordance with the terms of
              the agreement between the licensee and the sender unless the licensee
              \nhas a reasonable belief or a reasonable basis to believe that the
              sender may be a victim of fraud or \nthat a crime or violation of law,
              rule, or regulation has occurred, is occurring, or may occur. \n(b)
              \nIf a licensee fails to forward money received for transmission in
              accordance \nwith this section, the licensee must respond to inquiries
              by the sender with the reason for the failure \n \n22 Commentary: An
              express trust account or segregated bank account is not required.\n"

            on behalf of a licensee. \nSection 8.02 Unauthorized Activities \nA person
            shall not engage in the business of money transmission on behalf of a
            person \nnot licensed under this [Act]  or not exempt pursuant to [Article

            money transmission to the same extent as if the person were a \nlicensee,
            and shall be jointly and severally liable with the unlicensed or nonexempt

            \nThe [appropriate court] in an action brought by a licensee shall have
            \njurisdiction to grant appropriate equitable or legal relief, including
            without limitation prohibiting \nthe authorized delegate from directly
            or indirectly acting as an authorized delegate for any licensee \nin this
            state and the payment of restitution, damages or other monetary relief,
            if the [appropriate \ncourt] finds that an authorized delegate failed
            to remit money in accordance with the written \ncontract required by [Section

            \n(b) \nIf the [appropriate court] issues an order prohibiting a person
            from acting as \nan authorized delegate for any licensee pursuant to [Section
            8.03(a)], the licensee that brought the \naction shall report the order
            to the [Commissioner] within thirty (30) days and shall report the \norder
            through NMLS within ninety (90) days. \n(c) \nAn authorized delegate who
            holds money in trust for the benefit of a \nlicensee and knowingly fails
            to remit more than [$XXX] of such money is guilty of a [reference \nto
            state classification] felony. \n(d) \nAn authorized delegate who holds
            money in trust for the benefit of a \nlicensee and knowingly fails to
            remit no more than [$XXX] of such money is guilty of a [reference \nto
            state classification] misdemeanor. \nARTICLE IX  \nTimely Transmission,
            Refunds, and Disclosures \nSection 9.01 Timely Transmission. \n(a) \nEvery
            licensee shall forward all money received for transmission in \naccordance
            with the terms of the agreement between the licensee and the sender unless
            the licensee \nhas a reasonable belief or a reasonable basis to believe
            that the sender may be a victim of fraud or \nthat a crime or violation
            of law, rule, or regulation has occurred, is occurring, or may occur.
            \n(b) \nIf a licensee fails to forward money received for transmission
            in accordance \nwith this section, the licensee must respond to inquiries
            by the sender with the reason for the failure \n \n22 Commentary: An express
            trust account or segregated bank account is not required.\n"

          title: Reliance on Harbor’s prior identity verification
          why_it_matters: The plan would migrate 240,000 consumers and 12,000 business
            accounts without new verification. Harbor’s former vendor closed in 2024,
            and only pass or fail results are reported as available.

        - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c
          source_hash: 58b0379461c2b3d9c58807fff3ac3f7136d5aedd4f20022fc8be9eeb64cec409

          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md
          title: facts.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '# Known Facts


            - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
            out).

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance
            and full access to Mosaic''s product set.

            - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
            Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
            pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at
            Mosaic''s engine on day 1.

            - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

            - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were
            already KYC''d.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
            results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
            Relay''s engine on day 1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

            - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
            buys Harbor''s assets)

            - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
            institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

            - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
            last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
            Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its
            own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

            - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
            is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
            and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s
            AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must
            confirm.

            - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
            been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
            have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
            is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before
            onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume
            Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


            ## Assumptions


            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and
            Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition
            without new state approval

            - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
            Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

            - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
            validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state
            MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
            41 states (per the request).

            - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
            which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
            (structure not yet confirmed).

            '

          title: issues.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '# Issues and workstreams


            - Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
            the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
            the acquisition

            - BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing
            underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent
            CIP/AML obligations

            - SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
            ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability

            - Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring when
            the SaaS contract terminates at close

            - Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
            transmission or consumer protection implications

            - Customer account, contract, and data-transfer rights <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-af341445a7af4c06
            -->

            - Open alerts, suspicious activity history, and acquisition diligence
            <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-d22ea3957f21252a -->

            - State money-transmission authority after the asset purchase <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-65ae94508dcda1bf
            -->

            - Reliance on Harbor’s prior identity verification <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-30caa16a9234ccf2
            -->

            - Transaction-monitoring continuity at cutover <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-1d13a6636b8fbe60
            -->

            - Purchase-agreement authority and risk allocation <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-29efc151650e76a2
            -->

            - Customer terms, privacy, data transfers, and welcome bonus <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-fcd3954739e05481
            -->

            - Unresolved alerts, suspicious activity reports, and acquisition risk
            <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-9699bb16b60412ba -->

            - Reliance on Harbor identity checks and missing records <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-a6daa6bde380da74
            -->

            - Transaction-monitoring continuity at closing <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-034e6643b5481b97
            -->

            - Customer accounts, contracts, balances, data, and product access <!--
            issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-782d624fe465f404 -->

            - Acquisition terms, risk allocation, and migration authority <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4c1eee0ce2e8b327
            -->

            - Fifty-dollar welcome bonus <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-e96dfb10ae87aa47
            -->

            '

            - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
            Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
            pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at
            Mosaic''s engine on day 1.

            - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

            - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were
            already KYC''d.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
            results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
            Relay''s engine on day 1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

            - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
            buys Harbor''s assets)

            - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
            institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

            - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
            last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
            Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its
            own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

            - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
            is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
            and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s
            AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must
            confirm.

            - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
            been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
            have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
            is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before
            onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume
            Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


            ## Assumptions


            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and
            Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition
            without new state approval

            - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
            Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

            - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
            validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state
            MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
            41 states (per the request).

            - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
            which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
            (structure not yet confirmed).

            '

            - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
            buys Harbor''s assets)

            - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
            institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

            - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
            last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
            Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its
            own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

            - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
            is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
            and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s
            AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must
            confirm.

            - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
            been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
            have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
            is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before
            onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume
            Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


            ## Assumptions


            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and
            Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition
            without new state approval

            - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
            Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

            - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
            validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state
            MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
            41 states (per the request).

            - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
            which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
            (structure not yet confirmed).

            '

          support_state: supplied

          available_excerpt: '# Issues and workstreams


            - Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
            the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
            the acquisition

            - BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing
            underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent
            CIP/AML obligations

            - SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
            ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability

            - Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring when
            the SaaS contract terminates at close

            - Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
            transmission or consumer protection implications

            - Customer account, contract, and data-transfer rights <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-af341445a7af4c06
            -->

            - Open alerts, suspicious activity history, and acquisition diligence
            <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-d22ea3957f21252a -->

            - State money-transmission authority after the asset purchase <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-65ae94508dcda1bf
            -->

            - Reliance on Harbor’s prior identity verification <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-30caa16a9234ccf2
            -->

            - Transaction-monitoring continuity at cutover <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-1d13a6636b8fbe60
            -->

            - Purchase-agreement authority and risk allocation <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-29efc151650e76a2
            -->

            - Customer terms, privacy, data transfers, and welcome bonus <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-fcd3954739e05481
            -->

            - Unresolved alerts, suspicious activity reports, and acquisition risk
            <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-9699bb16b60412ba -->

            - Reliance on Harbor identity checks and missing records <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-a6daa6bde380da74
            -->

            - Transaction-monitoring continuity at closing <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-034e6643b5481b97
            -->

            - Customer accounts, contracts, balances, data, and product access <!--
            issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-782d624fe465f404 -->

            - Acquisition terms, risk allocation, and migration authority <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4c1eee0ce2e8b327
            -->

            - Fifty-dollar welcome bonus <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-e96dfb10ae87aa47
            -->

            '

          available_excerpt: "# Research this issue for the dossier: Customer terms,
            privacy, data\n\n## Question\n\nResearch this issue for the dossier: Customer
            terms, privacy, data transfers, and welcome bonus. Migration could change
            the service provider, account access, available products, data recipients,
            and customer rights. The proposed $50 bonus adds advertising and offer-term
            risk.\nLawyer's accepted priorities (private matter context):\nPrevent
            a monitoring gap and keep a staged-launch option available.: A failed
            cutover can create immediate compliance and customer harm. A controlled
            delay preserves reversibility.\nProposed date leads; resolve each reference
            in saved records before using it: [{\"role\": \"target acquisition close\",
            \"value\": \"Nov 15\", \"source_reference_id\": \"MSG-20260909-982278\",
            \"note\": \"The source describes this date as about ten weeks away. It
            does not expressly state the year or timezone.\"}, {\"role\": \"identity-verification
            vendor closure\", \"value\": \"2024\", \"source_reference_id\": \"MSG-20260909-982278\",
            \"note\": \"The record reports that Harbor\\u2019s identity-verification
            vendor closed in 2024.\"}]\n\n## Working Analysis\n\n**No external authority
            retrieved**\n\nI'll start by orienting in the matter records and the saved
            source library, then target the gaps that matter: customer contract assignment,
            privacy/data-transfer rules, and bonus marketing terms.\nNo oversized
            model request was sent.\n\nNo oversized model request was sent.\n\n##
            Sources surfaced\n\n- Supplied source: [facts.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c.md)
            [source:SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c]\n  Available excerpt:\n  > # Known
            Facts\n  > \n  > - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted
            Nov 15 (~10 weeks out).\n  > - Migration moves 240k consumer users and
            12k business accounts into Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-clos\n- Supplied
            source: [issues.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-db6468aeb552bb44aa7b.md)
            [source:SRC-LOCAL-db6468aeb552bb44aa7b]\n  Available excerpt:\n  > # Issues
            and workstreams\n  > \n  > - Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic
            Relay can operate under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor's
            MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition\n  > - BSA/AML an\n- Supplied source:
            [DOR-20260912-049f9c.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-8f6233be11b7205ffc25.md)
            [source:SRC-LOCAL-8f6233be11b7205ffc25]\n  Available excerpt:\n  > ##
            Preparation note\n  > \n  > **Result:** Legal and compliance review is
            required before the Nov 15 close. The migration is not only a technical
            data transfer.\n  > \n  > **Lawyer reframe:** Determine which pre-close
            an\n- Supplied source: [request.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c.md)
            [source:SRC-LOCAL-183892d6ee231d97ef4c]\n  Available excerpt:\n  > # Original
            Request\n  > \n  > From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration\n  > Subject:
            Harbor migration — anything here need legal?\n  > \n  > We're acquiring
            Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration plan:\n  >
            \n  > 240k\n\n## Last-mile work for counsel\n\n- Confirm the facts that
            could change the recommendation.\n- Verify any authority that will carry

          title: Transaction-monitoring continuity at cutover
          why_it_matters: Harbor’s monitoring feed will move to Mosaic’s system when
            Harbor’s current service ends at close. An incomplete change could create
            a gap in required monitoring.
          fact_ids: []

        - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c
          source_hash: 58b0379461c2b3d9c58807fff3ac3f7136d5aedd4f20022fc8be9eeb64cec409
          source_version: 58b0379461c2b3d9c58807fff3ac3f7136d5aedd4f20022fc8be9eeb64cec409
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md
          title: facts.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '# Known Facts


            - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
            out).

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance
            and full access to Mosaic''s product set.

            - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
            Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
            pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at
            Mosaic''s engine on day 1.

            - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

            - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were
            already KYC''d.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
            results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
            Relay''s engine on day 1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

            - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
            buys Harbor''s assets)

            - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
            institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

            - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
            last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
            Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its
            own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

            - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
            is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
            and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s
            AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must
            confirm.

            - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
            been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
            have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
            is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before
            onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume
            Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


            ## Assumptions


            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and
            Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition
            without new state approval

            - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
            Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

            - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
            validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state
            MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
            41 states (per the request).

            - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
            which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
            (structure not yet confirmed).

            '

            text: '# Known Facts

              out).

              - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and

              ## Assumptions

          source_version: 23fcb27105945705c2b19c1547c310303a49b7d909b07a52e0fe3323e0ae2e9a
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-8f6233be11b7205ffc25.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/dossier-requests/DOR-20260912-049f9c.md
          title: DOR-20260912-049f9c.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '## Preparation note


            **Result:** Legal and compliance review is required before the Nov 15
            close. The migration is not only a technical data transfer.


            **Lawyer reframe:** Determine which pre-close and day-one actions could
            change the deal risk, exceed Mosaic’s legal authority, weaken anti-money-laundering
            controls, or harm customers. Then separate safe preparation from actions
            that require legal clearance.


            The current plan relies on unconfirmed assumptions. Harbor’s licenses
            might not transfer in an asset purchase. Mosaic might not be able to rely
            on Harbor’s identity checks. About 40 alerts and several suspicious activity
            reports remain unreviewed. Harbor’s monitoring service will end at close,
            but the replacement has not been shown to work.


            The safer route is a staged launch. Mosaic can complete reversible technical
            work before close. It should delay affected accounts, new markets, or
            full product access if a legal or compliance gate remains open.


            ### Material issues


            - Unresolved alerts and suspicious activity history.

            - State money-transmission authority after the asset purchase.

            - Reliance on identity checks that lack underlying records.

            - Continuous transaction monitoring at cutover.

            - Purchase-agreement limits and allocation of compliance liabilities.

            - Customer terms, privacy notices, data transfers, and the $50 bonus.


            ### Three business priorities


            1. **Protect the acquisition decision.** Review unresolved compliance
            matters before Mosaic accepts risk that is difficult to reverse.

            2. **Confirm lawful day-one service.** Verify that Mosaic can serve each
            account and market after an asset purchase.

            3. **Preserve a safe fallback.** Keep monitoring active and permit a staged
            launch if any required control is not ready.


            ### First three issues to research


            1. **Open alerts and suspicious activity history.** The findings could
            change deal terms, closing conditions, remediation, or account eligibility.

            2. **State licensing.** Regulatory approval could take time and could
            prevent launch in the four new markets.

            3. **Identity-verification reliance.** Missing source records affect 240,000
            consumers and 12,000 business accounts and could require re-verification
            or access limits.




            > A requested saved-record read was unavailable. The writer used the material
            available.

            '

        - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-297efd7bb8430e22ef7b
          source_hash: 8da355f4654a8c9ef63ed45c11e80f7b1870be7b0eee84f77e33b55939d63b73
          source_version: 8da355f4654a8c9ef63ed45c11e80f7b1870be7b0eee84f77e33b55939d63b73
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-297efd7bb8430e22ef7b.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/dossier-requests/DOR-20260912-80f5fc.md
          title: DOR-20260912-80f5fc.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '## Harbor 2 — preparation note (close targeted Nov 15)


            **Controlling question:** does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition
            migration need legal review before the Nov 15 close? The newest turn ("Generate
            the Dossier") and the surrounding bank-held-funds comparisons are supporting
            work; they do not change that question. The record contains **no recorded
            decisions** at all, all five registered issues are `open`, and one intake
            question — how the $50 bonus is structured — was left open with no answer.


            **Lawyer reframe.** Dana''s framing is "it''s really just a data migration."
            The reframe is that the migration *is* the regulated event: on day 1 Mosaic
            Relay onboards 252,000 accounts into its own licensed product set with
            no re-verification, repoints monitoring, and launches a cash offer — under
            an asset-purchase structure in which nothing about Harbor''s licenses
            or compliance record transfers by default. The operative question is therefore
            not "can we inherit Harbor''s status," but **"what must Mosaic obtain,
            file, decide or preserve in its own name before Nov 15, and which parts
            of the stated deal upside are not actually available at close."**


            **Three premises the record lets us test**


            1. *"Just a data migration."* Fails. The day-1 cutover is the moment CIP,
            licensing, monitoring and disclosure obligations attach to 252k accounts.

            2. *"44 states is a nice win — opens four new markets."* Fails on the
            arithmetic and on the law. Harbor 38 + Mosaic 41 with a 44-state union
            yields **three** new states, not four, and the states are never named;
            and the plan to "use/assume" Harbor''s licenses rests on an assumption
            the record still marks open ([source:ASM-20260909-29b823]), against the
            saved position that transferability should not be relied on without a
            state rule, approval or written regulator position.

            3. *"No re-verification — they were already KYC''d."* Unresolved. Whether
            Mosaic''s own AML/CIP program permits reliance is unknown, and the pre-2023
            records cannot be recovered (vendor shut down in 2024; only pass/fail
            survives).


            **Different route the evidence supports.** Run the remaining ~9 weeks
            as a *gated launch*, not a documentation exercise: read and preserve the
            alert/SAR backlog; decide KYC reliance per cohort and schedule remediation;
            prove or drop the Harbor-only states; validate the monitoring cutover
            with a parallel run; paper notice/consent, new customer terms and closing
            conditions. If a gate fails, migrate only in the overlapping states on
            day 1 and onboard KYC-deficient accounts with limits — a scoped migration
            rather than the full one.


            **Material issues.** Five registered: money transmission licensing (44-state
            footprint); BSA/AML and KYC reliance for pre-2023 users; SAR and alert
            handling (~40 open alerts, unreviewed); monitoring vendor transition at
            close; $50 welcome bonus. Three that are missing and material: **migration
            data handling and customer notice** (the piece the request actually calls
            "just a data migration," never analyzed); **pre-close papering** (closing
            conditions, interim operation, allocation of the alert/SAR and KYC-record
            risk — no draft or terms appear anywhere); and **post-close access to
            Harbor''s alert/case history** (the records live in the SaaS system whose
            contract ends at close).


            **Three business priorities** — ranked by effect on the business question,
            time-fixedness and irreversibility, not by difficulty:


            1. **Read and preserve the alert/SAR backlog before anything else is scheduled.**
            It is the only item that can change the deal itself; it is entirely unread,
            so no one can yet say whether a filing is late or an account must be excluded;
            and the records needed to answer it may become inaccessible at close.

            2. **Decide KYC reliance for the pre-2023 cohort — rely, re-verify, or
            restrict access.** It determines whether "no re-verification" is available
            at all for the largest population; the underlying documents cannot be
            recreated; and re-verifying or limiting a cohort is a multi-week program
            that has to be decided before close, not after.

            3. **Prove the Harbor-only states can be served at close, or pull them
            from day-1 scope.** It tests the premise the deal is being sold on, state
            timelines are the longest external dependency and cannot be compressed,
            and serving a state without authority is a violation later paperwork cannot
            undo.


            **Research these first:** the SAR/alert backlog ([source:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38]),
            KYC reliance ([source:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045]), and the licensing
            position for the Harbor-only states ([source:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045])
            — in that order. If the team needs the earliest single go/no-go decision,
            licensing converts fastest — but it should still follow the backlog read,
            because a live SAR problem can outrank market access.


            **Watch-outs.** The bank-held-funds structure is a saved hypothetical
            and was never adopted; later turns ran under that scenario path, and some
            prior dossier drafts carry scenario scope, so their conclusions are not
            findings about this acquisition. The licensing research has **no citable
            support** (4 sources retrieved, 0 passages read; one output headed "No
            external authority retrieved"), and repeated "research queued/running"
            messages are status, not results. Assumption [source:ASM-20260909-57e86b]
            still calls the structure unconfirmed although the asset purchase is recorded
            and was reconfirmed twice by the user. The matter''s `target_date` field
            (2026-09-11) sits oddly against activity through 2026-09-12 — treat it
            as unverified. Urgent below marks the five items where the close date
            itself fixes the deadline and a mistake cannot be cured afterwards; it
            is not the same as the top-three ranking.


            *Not performed: no web research. Full-text reads covered the request,
            facts register, intake answers and early analysis; the licensing packet
            was reviewed at excerpt level and prior dossiers treated as fallible synthesis.*

            '

        - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c
          source_hash: 58b0379461c2b3d9c58807fff3ac3f7136d5aedd4f20022fc8be9eeb64cec409
          source_version: 58b0379461c2b3d9c58807fff3ac3f7136d5aedd4f20022fc8be9eeb64cec409
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-5b01121f7bfd7357746c.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md
          title: facts.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '# Known Facts


            - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
            out).

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance
            and full access to Mosaic''s product set.

            - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
            Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
            pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at
            Mosaic''s engine on day 1.

            - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

            - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
            Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

            - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were
            already KYC''d.

            - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
            results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
            onboarded before 2023.

            - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
            terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
            Relay''s engine on day 1.

            - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the
            last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

            - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
            buys Harbor''s assets)

            - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
            institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance

            - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
            last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
            Relay plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its
            own? — Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

            - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
            is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification
            and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s
            AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must
            confirm.

            - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
            been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
            have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.

            - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
            is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before
            onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume
            Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets.


            ## Assumptions


            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and
            Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition
            without new state approval

            - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic
            Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

            - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and
            validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state
            MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

            - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in
            41 states (per the request).

            - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
            which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
            (structure not yet confirmed).

            '

          available_excerpt: '# Issues and workstreams

            CIP/AML obligations

        - source_id: SRC-LOCAL-8f6233be11b7205ffc25
          source_hash: 23fcb27105945705c2b19c1547c310303a49b7d909b07a52e0fe3323e0ae2e9a
          source_version: 23fcb27105945705c2b19c1547c310303a49b7d909b07a52e0fe3323e0ae2e9a
          path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-LOCAL-8f6233be11b7205ffc25.md
          original_path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/dossier-requests/DOR-20260912-049f9c.md
          title: DOR-20260912-049f9c.md
          support_state: supplied
          source_type: workspace
          available_excerpt: '## Preparation note


            **Result:** Legal and compliance review is required before the Nov 15
            close. The migration is not only a technical data transfer.


            **Lawyer reframe:** Determine which pre-close and day-one actions could
            change the deal risk, exceed Mosaic’s legal authority, weaken anti-money-laundering
            controls, or harm customers. Then separate safe preparation from actions
            that require legal clearance.


            The current plan relies on unconfirmed assumptions. Harbor’s licenses
            might not transfer in an asset purchase. Mosaic might not be able to rely
            on Harbor’s identity checks. About 40 alerts and several suspicious activity
            reports remain unreviewed. Harbor’s monitoring service will end at close,
            but the replacement has not been shown to work.


            The safer route is a staged launch. Mosaic can complete reversible technical
            work before close. It should delay affected accounts, new markets, or
            full product access if a legal or compliance gate remains open.


            ### Material issues


            - Unresolved alerts and suspicious activity history.

            - State money-transmission authority after the asset purchase.

            - Reliance on identity checks that lack underlying records.

            - Continuous transaction monitoring at cutover.

            - Purchase-agreement limits and allocation of compliance liabilities.

            - Customer terms, privacy notices, data transfers, and the $50 bonus.


            ### Three business priorities


            1. **Protect the acquisition decision.** Review unresolved compliance
            matters before Mosaic accepts risk that is difficult to reverse.

            2. **Confirm lawful day-one service.** Verify that Mosaic can serve each
            account and market after an asset purchase.

            3. **Preserve a safe fallback.** Keep monitoring active and permit a staged
            launch if any required control is not ready.


            ### First three issues to research


            1. **Open alerts and suspicious activity history.** The findings could
            change deal terms, closing conditions, remediation, or account eligibility.

            2. **State licensing.** Regulatory approval could take time and could
            prevent launch in the four new markets.

            3. **Identity-verification reliance.** Missing source records affect 240,000
            consumers and 12,000 business accounts and could require re-verification
            or access limits.




            > A requested saved-record read was unavailable. The writer used the material
            available.

            '

          available_excerpt: '# Original Request


            From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

            Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


            We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
            plan:


            240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1
            post-close. One unified account, one balance, full access to our product
            set.

            No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

            Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice
            win, opens four new markets.

            Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
            results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
            before 2023.

            Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at
            close. Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day
            1.

            Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
            launching close+1.

            Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs
            from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


            Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

            '
          selected_passages:
          - start: 0
            end: 1095

          source_hash: 58b0379461c2b3d9c58807fff3ac3f7136d5aedd4f20022fc8be9eeb64cec409
